Optimization loop:
```
def projected_subgradient(h, T, steps, alpha, lr, momentum, topK, deadline):
    y = h.copy(); v=0.
    for step in range(steps):
       q = correlate(y,1-y)*dx
       m=q.max()
       diff=alpha*(q-m)
       w=softmax(diff)
       # top K
       select = np.argsort(w)[-K:] # or top q
       ws = w[select]; renormalize
       g = zero
       for p in select: s=p-(n-1); add ws * grad_lag(s)
       g += (changet?) no equality handled by projection
       v = beta*v - lr*g
       y = y + v
       h = project(y)
       F = smoothmax h (maybe evaluate exact C periodically)
       line search? Simple fixed lr with adaptive step based on Lipschitz? Use backtracking: after proposed h, reduce if F increases.
```

```
A = np.zeros((n,L))
s = np.arange(-(n-1), n)
for i in range(n):
    pos = i + s
    m = (pos>=0)&(pos<n)
    A[i,m] += 1 - h[pos[m]]
    pos2 = i - s
    m2=(pos2>=0)&(pos2<n)
    A[i,m2] -= h[pos2[m2]]
A[:, n-1]=0.0
```

Pseudo for binary local search:
```
def binary_prepare(h, n):
    q = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full').astype(float)
    A = compute_A(h,n)
    curC = q.max()*dx
    return q,A,curC

def update_A_swap(A, i, j, n):
    # assume h_i 0->1, h_j 1->0
    L=2*n-1
    r = np.arange(n)
    # for i delta +1:
    col = n-1 + i - r
    valid = (col>=0)&(col<L)
    A[ r[valid], col[valid] ] -= 1  # plus term -Δ (Δ=1)
    col2 = n-1 + r - i
    valid2 = ...
    A[ r[valid2], col2[valid2] ] += 1
    # for j delta -1:
    col = n-1 + j - r
    A[... ] += 1? Since Δ=-1: plus -Δ = +1
    col2 = n-1 + r - j
    A[...] -= 1?
    A[:, n-1]=0
```
Need update q using old A before updates: delta = A[i] - A[j]; plus1 at p1,p2; q += delta; A update. Then h update. But A_i/A_j old; if we update q after A update? Store.

```
ones = np.where(h==1)[0]
Ai = A[i]

# zeros row i not in ones; p1,p2 calculations relative to ones array
rows = np.arange(m)
p1 = n-1 + ones - i
# p1 valid? ones-i between -(n-1), n-1 always yes; but if p1 == n-1 (ones==i) not possible.
W[rows,p1] += 1
p2 = n-1 + i - ones
W[rows,p2] += 1
cand = W.max(axis=1)

```
def compute_A(h,n):
 L=2*n-1; s=np.arange(-(n-1),n)
 A=np.zeros((n,L))
 for i in range(n):
   pos=i+s; m=(pos>=0)&(pos<n)
   A[i,m] += 1.0 - h[pos[m]]
   pos2=i-s; m2=(pos2>=0)&(pos2<n)
   A[i,m2] -= h[pos2[m2]]
 A[:, n-1]=0.0
 return A
```

def project_box_sum(y, n):

```
def binary_local_from_state(h, n, deadline, max_sweeps=100000, restart_perturb=False):
    # h binary or thresholded
    h = make_binary(h,n)
    q,A,curC = binary_init(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_C=curC
    sweeps=0
    while time.time()<deadline and sweeps<max_sweeps:
       improved=False
       # order zeros maybe random or original
       zeros = np.where(h==0)[0]
       ones = np.where(h==1)[0]
       # To exploit changes, for i in zeros:
       for i in zeros:
          if h[i]!=0: continue
          if time... break
          m = len(ones)
          if m==0: break
          # candidate W
          W = q[None,:] - A[ones]  # This allocates and copies. Could be optimized: V = -A[ones]; V += q? 
          W += A[i][None,:]
          rows=np.arange(m)
          p1 = n-1 + ones - i
          W[rows, p1] += 1
          p2 = n-1 + i - ones
          W[rows, p2] += 1
          cand = W.max(axis=1)
          # choose best j if improves
          best_idx = np.argmin(cand)
          if cand[best_idx] * dx < curC - 1e-12:
              j = ones[best_idx]
              apply_swap(h,A,q,i,j,n)
              curC = q.max()*dx  # or track from cand? After apply, q updated, max can be computed O(L)
              if curC < best_C: best_C=curC; best_h=h.copy()
              improved=True
              # ones and zeros sets change; easiest break for loop to recompute? Or update dynamic:
              # If break, sweep restarts? Could be slow but okay. Better continue with updated structures? We can update ones array by replacing j with i? But zeros list old. Simpler break inner for and go to next sweep after one improvement. First-improvement. But best-improvement per sweep might be stronger. We can recompute zeros/ones after each improvement within sweep? Might be heavy but okay n small. Could set `started` and restart for loop? 
       if not improved: break
       sweeps +=1
    return best_h,best_C
```

Functions:
```
def q_and_smooth(h,n,alpha,topK):
    q = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full') * (2.0/n)
    m=q.max()
    sh=alpha*(q-m)
    F = m + np.log(np.exp(sh).sum())/alpha
    # weights
    w = np.exp(sh); w /= w.sum()
    # topK indices
    if topK < len(w):
        idx = np.argpartition(w, -topK)[-topK:]
        ww = w[idx]; ww /= ww.sum()
        sel=idx
    else sel, ww = np.arange(len), w
    return F, q, sel, ww
```

s = np.arange(-(n-1),n)
for i in range(n):
 pos = i+s; m=(pos>=0)&(pos<n); B[i,m] += 1 - h[pos[m]]

# for s=0, B[i,n-1] = 1-2h_i (already set by pos and pos2 both valid: pos=i add1-h_i, pos2=i subtract h_i =>1-2h_i)
```

```
def gradient_smooth(x, q, sel, w, n, topK):
    dx=2.0/n
    g=np.zeros(n)
    center=n-1
    for idx, ww in zip(sel, w):
        s = idx - center
        if s == 0:
            g += ww*(1.0 - 2.0*x)
        elif s > 0:
            # first: i=0..n-s-1 add 1 - x[i+s]
            g[:n-s] += ww*(1.0 - x[s:])
            # second: i=s..n-1 subtract x[i-s]
            g[s:] -= ww*x[:-s]
        else:
            t = -s
            # first: i=t..n-1, i+s >=0 but <n? i+s max n-1+s? Since s negative, i+s <= n-1+t? Valid if i+s < n automatically for i<n? For s=-t, i-s? Let's derive:
            # first term valid if 0≤i+s<n => t≤i≤n-1 and i-s=i+t≤n-1? i+t≤n-1? Wait s negative, i+s = i-t. Need 0≤i-t<n => t≤i≤n-1+t? The upper is i<t? No i+t? Let's do general:
            # For s>0: i+s<n => i<n-s; i+s>=0 ok. second i-s>=0 => i>=s.
            # For s<0 (s=-t): first i+s>=0 => i>=t; i+s<n => i<n + t (always for i≤n-1 if t>0? i-t≤n-1 always since i≤n-1, yes). So i=t..n-1, add 1 - x[i - t].
            # second i-s = i+t < n => i<n-t; i+s>=0? not relevant for h index; valid if i-s>=0 (i>=-t always). So i=0..n-t-1, subtract x[i+t].
            g[t:] += ww*(1.0 - x[:n-t])
            g[:n-t] -= ww*x[t:]
```
Check s<0: q_s = ∑_{i=t}^{n-1} h_i(1-h_{i-t}). Derivative w.r.t h_i: first term for i as first: (1-h_{i-t}); second term for i as second: previous index i+t: subtract h_{i+t}. Good. So g[t:]+=1-h[:n-t]; g[:n-t]-=h[t:]. Good.
For s>0: first i=0..n-s-1: 1-h[s:]; second i=s..n-1: subtract x[:-s]. Good.

Projected gradient exact sum as a fallback/polish:
```
def projected_gradient_polish(h,n,deadline,alpha=100,steps=2000,momentum=0.7,lr=0.05):
   h=project; best=...
   v=np.zeros(n); step=lr
   for it in range(steps):
     F,q,sel,w = smooth_data
     g=grad
     v=momentum*v - step*g
     for trial in [1,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625]:
        hp=project(h+trial*v)
        Fp=smooth_only
        if Fp < F - 1e-14: break
     else: step*=0.5; v=0; hp=h
     h=hp
     v=... maybe if rejected set v=0
     c=compute exact; update best
   return best
```

Let's consider a simplex/LP approach for fractional local. Since q_k are bilinear, but if we fix the values `r = 1-h`? Alternating: For fixed r, minimize max ∑ a_i b_i linear in h. This is a convex LP: minimize t s.t. ∑ h_i A_{k,i} ≤ t, 0≤h≤1, sum=1. We can solve LP efficiently with scipy.optimize.linprog (method='highs'). Then set h_new = argmin; then r=1-h; repeat. This is natural for minmax bilinear? It approximates by alternating linearization, maybe finds better global. Let's explore.

Given current h (thus r=1-h), objective as function of h: q_k = ∑ h_i r_{i+s}. For fixed r≥0, each q_k linear in h. We want minimize max_k q_k subject to bounds/sum. This is a convex LP. The optimal h for fixed r will be at extreme (some 0/1) but feasible. Then update r=1-h. Next iteration minimize max linear with new r. This is a fixed-point/alternating procedure. Does it converge to local min of original? It's like alternating minimization of max_k ∑ h_i(1-h_{i+s}) where for fixed complement r it's linear. Since for fixed r, min over h of q_k; for fixed h, min over r? If we also can optimize r (with r=1-h, sum r = n/2), alternating is exact: problem min_h max_k ∑ h_i r_{i+s} with r=1-h. Let's examine LP local more. At optimum of original, first-order KKT with respect to active constraints: for active set A with weights λ, gradient G = ∑λ[(1-h_{i+s}) - h_{i-s}] + μ =0 (ignoring bounds). The LP fixed r uses gradient (1-h_old_{i+s}) only, missing -h_old_i (due to r change). Moving h changes r and thus second-order. If h near 0/1, h_old_i is 0/1; missing term significant. But LP model at h_old has correct q. For a direction Δ, model q change = ∑ Δ_i r_{i+s}; true q change = model change - ∑ h_i Δ_{i+s}? Let's expand true q(h+Δ)=∑ (h_i+Δ_i)(1-h_{i+s}-Δ_{i+s}) = q_old +∑Δ_i(1-h_{i+s}) - ∑ h_i Δ_{i+s} -∑Δ_iΔ_{i+s}. The model includes only first term. The second term is -∑ h_j Δ_j (shifted weights), which is the negative gradient from second factor. For binary h, this can be large. So LP with r fixed is only one-sided linearization; it may not respect optimum conditions. Alternating: after h changes, r changes, and next LP uses new r. It might still converge to a fixed point where each step minimizes one-sided model. Not sure.

We could make LP model more accurate by linearizing both h and r: Use first-order Taylor of q_k around h_old:
q_k ≈ q0_k + ∇q_k(h_old)·Δ. Minimize max over k of q0_k + ∇q_k·Δ subject to h_old+Δ in box/sum. This is a convex LP with variables h (or Δ) and t: t ≥ q0_k + ∇q_k·(h-h_old). This exactly uses full gradient (both terms). This is better! Let's implement LP trust using first-order (quasi-Newton) linearization rather than fixed r.

Constraints: For each lag k, (∇q_k)·h ≤ t - q0_k + ∇q_k·h_old. Let d_k = ∇q_k·h_old - q0_k. Then t ≥ q0 + ∇·(h-h_old). Or A_k h ≤ b_k where b_k = t? Include t: A_k h - t ≤ q0_k + ∇·h_old - q0_k = ∇·h_old - q0_k. Wait:
q0 + ∇(h-h0) ≤ t => ∇h - t ≤ ∇h0 - q0.
Coefficients A_k = ∇q_k (length n), RHS b_k = ∇q_k·h0 - q0_k. This LP linearization about h0. For h=h0, constraint RHS = q0? Check: LHS ∇h0 - t; need ≤ ∇h0-q0 => t ≥ q0. Good.
This LP is convex, uses true first-order. It incorporates changing r. Great. We can solve min t subject to linearized constraints, box around h0, sum. This is a standard linearization trust region for minimax of smooth/nonsmooth functions. It may significantly improve and is consistent with gradient.

Can we derive B matrix faster? For SLP, B[i,p] = derivative of unnormalized q_{s} wrt h_i. We can build as earlier loop over i. But for LP constraints A rows = B.T, so B column p corresponds lag p. Building B[i,pos] rows. Implementation:
```
def build_derivative_matrix(h,n):
 L=2*n-1; s=np.arange(-(n-1),n)
 B=np.zeros((n,L), dtype=float)
 for i in range(n):
    pos=i+s; m=(pos>=0)&(pos<n); B[i,m] += 1.0 - h[pos[m]]
    pos2=i-s; m2=(pos2>=0)&(pos2<n); B[i,m2] -= h[pos2[m2]]
 return B
```
For s=0, both valid at p=n-1: adds 1-h_i and subtracts h_i =>1-2h_i, correct. This uses h_i in derivative; good. For binary local A, we zero s=0 because finite swap; for derivative not.

But for q_s where s negative, B formula with pos=i+s and pos2=i-s works? Example s=-t: pos=i-t valid if i≥t -> add 1-h[i-t]; pos2=i+t valid if i<n-t -> subtract h[i+t]. Good.

This loop uses `np.arange` each i; we can precompute for n: `i_plus_valid_indices[i]`, `i_minus_valid_indices[i]`, and maybe s arrays. But n small. Could precompute globally for performance. Top-level function to get cached? No global mutable? We can use function attribute cache? Maybe acceptable. But rules no closures, but functions can have attributes. Simpler inside function compute `s` each; for SLP iterations 100, 100*250*500 loops okay. To speed, in SLP, we can maintain B incrementally? When h changes slightly, B changes for all rows because derivative depends on h at other indices. Updating all B would be O(nL) anyway. Computing from scratch O(nL) fine.

def project_box_sum(y,n):
    y=np.asarray(y,float)
    target=n*0.5
    if y.shape[0]!=n: ... maybe resize? not.
    lo=min(y.min()-1.0, -np.inf?) Use y_min-1, y_max+1.
    for _ in range(100):
      tau=(lo+hi)/2
      h=np.clip(y-tau,0,1)
      s=h.sum()
      if s>target: lo=tau
      else: hi=tau
    h=np.clip(y-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
    # adjust residual
    for _ in range(20):
      r=target-h.sum()
      if abs(r)<1e-14: break
      if r>0:
        room=1-h
        sr=room.sum()
        if sr<=1e-14: break
        h=h+r*room/sr
      else:
        sm=h.sum()
        if sm<=1e-14: break
        h=h+r*h/sm
    return np.clip(h,0,1)
```
If y has NaN? no.

def adjust_in_box(h, lower, upper, n):
   h = np.clip(h, lower, upper) maybe? But lower/upper arrays target sum.
   target=n/2

     r=target-h.sum()

     if r>0:
       room=upper-h
       room[room<1e-12]=0
       sr=room.sum()
       if sr<1e-12:
          # add uniformly to all not upper
          mask=upper-h>1e-9; if none break
          m=mask.sum(); h[mask]+=r/m
       else: h+=r*room/sr
     else similar subtract from h-lower

- `compute_binary_A(h,n)` (finite increment, s=0 zero)
```
def compute_binary_A(h,n):
  L=2*n-1; s=np.arange(-(n-1),n)
  A=np.zeros((n,L))
  for i in range(n):
    pos=i+s; m=(pos>=0)&(pos<n); A[i,m] += 1.0 - h[pos[m]]
    pos2=i-s; m2=(pos2>=0)&(pos2<n); A[i,m2] -= h[pos2[m2]]
  A[:, n-1]=0.0
  return A
```
This works for binary h. For fractional rounded, h exactly 0/1.

SLP LP implementation details:
```
def build_derivative_matrix(h,n): as above.
def slp_step(h,n,delta):
   q = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')*dx
   C = q.max()
   B = build_derivative_matrix(h,n)  # unnormalized derivatives
   A_rows = B.T * dx  # L x n
   b = A_rows @ h - q  # length L
   A_ub = np.empty((L,n+1)); A_ub[:,:n]=A_rows; A_ub[:,n]=-1
   b_ub = b
   A_eq = np.ones((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,n]=0
   b_eq = np.array([n/2])
   c = np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1
   lower = np.clip(h-delta,0,1); upper=np.clip(h+delta,0,1)
   bounds = list(zip(lower,upper)); bounds.append((0.0,None))
   sol = linprog(c,A_ub,b_ub,A_eq,b_eq,bounds,method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
   if not sol.success: return None
   h_new = sol.x[:n]
   # enforce box/sum via adjust_in_box with lower,upper
   h_new = adjust_in_box(h_new, lower, upper, n)
   C_new = compute_c5(h_new,n)
   return h_new, C_new
```

def lbfgb_fun(x,n,alpha,lam,topK):
   q = np.correlate(x,1-x,'full')*(2/n)
   m=q.max(); sh=alpha*(q-m); logs = ... # avoid overflow
   F = m + np.log(np.exp(sh).sum())/alpha
   res = x.sum()-n/2
   return F + lam*res*res

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
  t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-5
  rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
  # initialize
  try:
    parent=np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
    n=len(parent)
  except:
    n=250; parent=np.full(n,0.5)
  if n%2!=0: n=n+1? For even binary? But if parent odd, project and keep; for binary use n_adj even by resize.
  h0=project_box_sum(parent,n)
  best_h=h0.copy(); best_C=compute_c5(h0,n); best_n=n
  # define update helper? no closures. We'll inline.
  # 1. quick SLP from parent
  h_slp, C_slp = slp_refine(best_h, best_n, min(deadline, t0+0.35*budget_s), initial_delta=0.1, max_iters=2000, rng=rng)
  update
  # 2. binary local from rounded best
  h_bin = make_binary(best_h,best_n) if best_n%2==0
  C_bin=...
  h_bin2, C_bin2 = binary_local_search(h_bin,best_n,min(deadline, t0+0.5*budget_s), rng)
  update
  # SLP from best (which may be fractional/binary)
  ...
  # 3. alternate multi-res
  for nh in [300, 350, 200, 400]:
     if time > deadline-300: break
     hh=resize_h(best_h,best_n,nh); project
     # binary local quick if nh even
     # SLP quick
     # lbfgs quick
     update
  # 4. final: SLP with small delta on best, binary local, LBFGS until deadline
  ...
  # final projection and exact eval
  best_h=project_box_sum(best_h,best_n)
  best_C=compute_c5(best_h,best_n)
  return best_h,best_C,best_n
```

Implement `slp_refine(h,n,deadline, max_iters=5000, initial_delta=0.15, min_delta=1e-5, allow_perturb=True, rng=None)`:
```
def slp_refine(h,n,deadline, max_iters=5000, initial_delta=0.15, min_delta=1e-5, rng=None):
    h=project_box_sum(h,n); C=compute_c5(h,n); best_h=h.copy(); best_C=C
    delta=initial_delta; stall=0; fails=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       if delta < min_delta: break
       res = slp_step(h,n,delta)
       if res is None:
          fails+=1; delta*=0.5;
          if fails>10: return best...
          continue
       h_new,C_new=res
       if C_new < C - 1e-12:
          h=h_new; C=C_new; if C<best_C: best...
          stall=0; fails=0
          delta=max(delta*0.8, min_delta)
       else:
          stall+=1
          delta*=0.5
          # after many stalls, perturb around best
          if rng is not None and stall > 10:
             noise_scale = min(delta*0.5, 0.02)
             h = best_h + noise_scale*rng.randn(n)
             h = project_box_sum(h,n)
             C = compute_c5(h,n)
             delta = max(min(0.05, noise_scale*2), min_delta)
             stall=0
    # final exact from best
    return best_h,best_C
```
But if starting from a perturbation worse, SLP may not improve relative to current C; best remains. We can set h to perturbed and C; if SLP finds below best, update. Good.

    dx=2.0/n
    q=np.correlate(x,1-x,'full')*dx
    m=q.max()
    sh=alpha*(q-m)

```
def lbfgs_polish(h,n,deadline):
    h=project_box_sum(h,n); C=compute_c5; best...
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    alphas=[30,150,800,4000]
    lams=[0.1,10,1000]
    for alpha in alphas:
      for lam in lams:
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        maxit=300
        try:
          res=minimize(lbfgb_fun, h, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=lbfgb_jac, args=(n,alpha,lam), bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxit,'ftol':1e-18,'gtol':1e-12,'maxls':20})
        except Exception:
          continue
        h_new=project_box_sum(res.x,n)
        c=compute_c5(h_new,n)
        if c < C - 1e-12:
           h=h_new; C=c
        if c < best_C: best...
    return best_h,best_C
```

`adjust_in_box`: Suppose r positive, room sum zero (all at upper). But feasibility old h target should be within; if not, cannot. We can fallback to project_box_sum ignoring box? But for LP step, if infeasible, maybe reject. Implement:
```
def adjust_in_box(h, lower, upper, n):
    target=n*0.5
    h=np.clip(h, lower, upper)
    for _ in range(100):
       r=target-h.sum()
       if abs(r)<1e-12: break
       if r>0:
          room=upper-h
          idx=room>1e-12
          sr=room[idx].sum()
          if sr<=1e-12:
             # add uniformly where not upper
             h[idx]+=r/np.count_nonzero(idx) if any else 0
          else:
             h[idx]+=r*room[idx]/sr
       else:
          space=h-lower
          idx=space>1e-12
          ss=space[idx].sum()
          if ss<=1e-12:
             h[idx]+=r/np.count_nonzero(idx)
          else:
             h[idx]+=r*space[idx]/ss
    return np.clip(h, lower, upper)
```
If `idx` empty and r not tiny, returning clipped wrong sum. Could detect and fallback `project_box_sum`, but may violate box. In SLP, if box infeasible (shouldn't), we can return None. We'll have adjust return None if not feasible.

final r small; return

Let's implement `adjust_in_box` robust:
```
def adjust_in_box(h, lower, upper, n):
    target=n*0.5
    h=np.array(h,float)
    # If target outside interval sums, use project and return None if bad
    if h.sum() ...
    for _ in range(200):
       h=np.clip(h,lower,upper)
       r=target-h.sum()
       if abs(r)<1e-12: return h
       if r>0:
          room=upper-h
          mask=room>1e-12
          if not mask.any(): return None
          s=room[mask].sum()
          # If adding proportion would exceed some upper due to rounding, use min; add again after clipping
          h[mask]+=r*room[mask]/s
       else:
          fall=h-lower
          mask=fall>1e-12
          if not mask.any(): return None
          s=fall[mask].sum()
          h[mask]+=r*fall[mask]/s
    h=np.clip(h,lower,upper)
    if abs(target-h.sum())>1e-7:
       return project_box_sum(h,n) # maybe violates but better
    return h
```
Proportional addition won't exceed upper because max add to index with max room r*room_i/sum_room ≤ room_i if r ≤ sum_room. If r > sum_room due to infeasible, then would exceed; return None maybe. Use if r > sum_room: return None. For r<0 similar. Good.

Let's implement orientation-aware functions:
```
def build_derivative_matrix(h,n):
    L=2*n-1
    s=np.arange(-(n-1), n)
    B=np.zeros((n,L), dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        pos = i - s
        m = (pos>=0)&(pos<n)
        B[i,m] += 1.0 - h[pos[m]]
        pos2 = i + s
        m2 = (pos2>=0)&(pos2<n)
        B[i,m2] -= h[pos2[m2]]
    return B
```
For k=0, pos=i, pos2=i, B=1-2h.

# plus affected col_plus = center + r - a; A[r,col_plus] += -d
# minus affected col_minus = center + a - r; A[r,col_minus] += d
```

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog
import time

def compute_c5(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    q=np.correlate(h,1.0-h,mode='full')
    return float(np.max(q)*(2.0/n)) if n>0 else 0.0
...

def get_position_struct(n):
    cache = getattr(get_position_struct, 'cache', None)
    if cache is None:
        cache = {}
        get_position_struct.cache = cache
    if n in cache: return cache[n]
    L=2*n-1; s=np.arange(-(n-1),n)
    plus_cols=[]; plus_pos=[]; minus_cols=[]; minus_pos=[]
    for i in range(n):
       pos=i-s
       m=(pos>=0)&(pos<n)
       plus_cols.append(np.nonzero(m)[0]); plus_pos.append(pos[m])
       pos2=i+s
       m2=(pos2>=0)&(pos2<n)
       minus_cols.append(np.nonzero(m2)[0]); minus_pos.append(pos2[m2])
    struct=(plus_cols,plus_pos,minus_cols,minus_pos)
    cache[n]=struct
    return struct
```

`apply_binary_swap`:
```
def apply_binary_swap(h,A,q,i,j,n,r):
    center=n-1
    delta = A[i] - A[j]
    delta[center + (i - j)] += 1.0
    delta[center + (j - i)] += 1.0
    q += delta
    d1 = 1.0
    A[r, center + r - i] -= d1
    A[r, center + i - r] -= d1
    d2 = -1.0
    A[r, center + r - j] -= d2   # adds 1
    A[r, center + j - r] -= d2
    A[:, center] = 0.0
    h[i]=1.0; h[j]=0.0
```
Check signs: For j d=-1, subtract d = +1. Good.

But note: The order of updating A with i and j simultaneous; since updates are linear and independent, order doesn't matter. However the A row i and j themselves might be updated by the other's change: When i changes, A_j rows? Yes A rows all updated. We compute delta before. Good.

Now, implement `lbfgs_polish`. It calls minimize many times; if `lbfgb_jac` expensive, okay. But L-BFGS may produce x with sum far; penalty lam sequence. After each, project and if C improved best. It may degrade working h if exact C worse; we can set working h to best if no improvement? In loop, `h` starts best. After minimize, if c_new < C, set h to new. Else keep old h (not res.x). Also update best if c_new<best_C. This avoids degrading. Good.
```
def lbfgs_polish(h,n,deadline):
   h=project; C=compute; best...
   for alpha in [80,400,2000]:
     for lam in [1.0,100.0,10000.0]:
       if time>deadline break
       try res=minimize(..., h, ...)
       h_new=project(res.x)
       c=...
       if c < C: h=h_new; C=c
       if c<best_C: ...
       # if res.x sum bad but c better, okay after project
   return best
```
But if lam small and sum off, c may appear better due to penalty? We use objective includes penalty, but c exact may be worse. We compare exact c. Good.

 update best

 if C<best_C update

   else: bn=best_n+1; bh=make_binary(resize_h(best_h,best_n,bn),bn)

 return best_h, float(best_C), int(best_n)

```python
import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog


def compute_c5(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if n <= 0 or h.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    q = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")
    return float(np.max(q) * (2.0 / n))


def project_box_sum(y, n):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    if y.size != n:
        if y.size > n:
            y = y[:n]
        else:
            tmp = np.zeros(n)
            m = min(n, y.size)
            tmp[:m] = y[:m]
            y = tmp
    target = 0.5 * n
    if y.size != n:
        y = np.resize(y, n)

    lo = float(np.min(y)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(y)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(80):
        tau = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        h = np.clip(y - tau, 0.0, 1.0)
        s = float(h.sum())
        if s > target:
            lo = tau
        else:
            hi = tau
    h = np.clip(y - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

    for _ in range(20):
        r = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(r) < 1e-14:
            break
        if r > 0.0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            sr = float(room.sum())
            if sr <= 1e-14:
                break
            h = h + (r / sr) * room
        else:
            sm = float(h.sum())
            if sm <= 1e-14:
                break
            h = h + (r / sm) * h
    return np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)


def adjust_in_box(h, lower, upper, n):
    if n <= 0:
        return h
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).reshape(-1).copy()
    lower = np.asarray(lower, dtype=float)
    upper = np.asarray(upper, dtype=float)

    for _ in range(120):
        h = np.clip(h, lower, upper)
        r = target - float(h.sum())
        if abs(r) < 1e-12:
            return h
        if r > 0.0:
            room = upper - h
            mask = room > 1e-12
            if not np.any(mask):
                return None
            s = float(room[mask].sum())
            if r > s + 1e-12:
                return None
            h[mask] += (r / s) * room[mask]
        else:
            fall = h - lower
            mask = fall > 1e-12
            if not np.any(mask):
                return None
            s = float(fall[mask].sum())
            if -r > s + 1e-12:
                return None
            h[mask] += (r / s) * fall[mask]

    h = np.clip(h, lower, upper)
    if abs(target - float(h.sum())) > 1e-7:
        return None
    return h


def snap_sum(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).reshape(-1).copy()
    if n <= 0:
        return h
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    r = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(r) > 1e-15:
        if r > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            if 1.0 - h[idx] + 1e-15 >= r:
                h[idx] += r
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            if h[idx] + 1e-15 >= -r:
                h[idx] += r
        h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    return h


def get_position_struct(n):
    cache = getattr(get_position_struct, "cache", None)
    if cache is None:
        cache = {}
        get_position_struct.cache = cache
    if n in cache:
        return cache[n]

    L = 2 * n - 1
    s = np.arange(-(n - 1), n)
    plus_cols = []
    plus_pos = []
    minus_cols = []
    minus_pos = []

    for i in range(n):
        pos = i - s
        m = (pos >= 0) & (pos < n)
        cols = np.nonzero(m)[0]
        plus_cols.append(cols)
        plus_pos.append(pos[m])

        pos2 = i + s
        m2 = (pos2 >= 0) & (pos2 < n)
        cols2 = np.nonzero(m2)[0]
        minus_cols.append(cols2)
        minus_pos.append(pos2[m2])

    struct = (plus_cols, plus_pos, minus_cols, minus_pos)
    cache[n] = struct
    return struct


def build_derivative_matrix(h, n):
    """Derivative matrix B for np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full').

    B[i, p] is d q_p / d h_i, where q_p is the unnormalized correlation.
    """
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    plus_cols, plus_pos, minus_cols, minus_pos = get_position_struct(n)
    B = np.zeros((n, L), dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        pc = plus_cols[i]
        pp = plus_pos[i]
        if pc.size:
            B[i, pc] += 1.0 - h[pp]
        mc = minus_cols[i]
        mp = minus_pos[i]
        if mc.size:
            B[i, mc] -= h[mp]
    return B


def compute_binary_A(h, n):
    """Finite 0->1 increment deltas for binary h under np.correlate ordering."""
    L = 2 * n - 1
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    plus_cols, plus_pos, minus_cols, minus_pos = get_position_struct(n)
    A = np.zeros((n, L), dtype=float)
    for i in range(n):
        pc = plus_cols[i]
        pp = plus_pos[i]
        if pc.size:
            A[i, pc] += 1.0 - h[pp]
        mc = minus_cols[i]
        mp = minus_pos[i]
        if mc.size:
            A[i, mc] -= h[mp]
    A[:, n - 1] = 0.0
    return A


def apply_binary_swap(h, A, q, i, j, n, r):
    center = n - 1
    delta = A[i] - A[j]
    delta[center + (i - j)] += 1.0
    delta[center + (j - i)] += 1.0
    q += delta

    # Update A for h_i += 1 and h_j -= 1.
    A[r, center + r - i] -= 1.0
    A[r, center + i - r] -= 1.0
    A[r, center + r - j] += 1.0
    A[r, center + j - r] += 1.0
    A[:, center] = 0.0

    h[i] = 1.0
    h[j] = 0.0


def make_binary(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    if h.size != n:
        h = resize_h(h, h.size, n)
    target = n // 2
    hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if target > 0 and n > 0:
        idx = np.argsort(-h, kind="stable")[:target]
        hb[idx] = 1.0
    return hb


def binary_local_search(h, n, deadline, rng=None):
    if n <= 0 or n % 2 != 0:
        return h, compute_c5(h, n)
    dx = 2.0 / n
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    target = n // 2
    if not np.isclose(float(h.sum()), target, rtol=1e-12, atol=1e-10):
        h = make_binary(h, n)

    if np.count_nonzero(h == 0.0) == 0 or np.count_nonzero(h == 1.0) == 0:
        h = make_binary(h, n)

    q = np.asarray(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full"), dtype=float)
    A = compute_binary_A(h, n)
    curC = float(np.max(q) * dx)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_C = curC
    r = np.arange(n)

    while time.time() < deadline:
        zeros = np.where(h == 0.0)[0]
        if zeros.size == 0:
            break
        ones = np.where(h == 1.0)[0]
        if ones.size == 0:
            break

        improved = False
        for i in zeros:
            if h[i] != 0.0:
                continue
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break

            m = ones.size
            if m == 0:
                break

            W = q[None, :] - A[ones]
            W += A[i][None, :]
            rows = np.arange(m)
            p1 = (n - 1) + (ones - i)
            p2 = (n - 1) + (i - ones)
            W[rows, p1] += 1.0
            W[rows, p2] += 1.0

            cand = W.max(axis=1)
            best_pos = int(np.argmin(cand))
            if cand[best_pos] * dx < curC - 1e-12:
                j = int(ones[best_pos])
                apply_binary_swap(h, A, q, int(i), j, n, r)
                curC = float(np.max(q) * dx)
                if curC < best_C:
                    best_C = curC
                    best_h = h.copy()
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            break

    best_C = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_C


def resize_h(h, n_old, n_new):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).reshape(-1)
    if n_old <= 0 or n_new <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n_new, dtype=float)
    if n_old == n_new:
        return h.copy()
    x_old = (np.arange(n_old) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n_old)
    x_new = (np.arange(n_new) + 0.5) * (2.0 / n_new)
    hh = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h, left=0.0, right=0.0)
    return project_box_sum(hh, n_new)


def slp_step(h, n, delta, max_rows=None):
    if n <= 0:
        return h.copy(), compute_c5(h, 0)
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    ndx = 2.0 / n
    q = np.asarray(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full"), dtype=float) * ndx
    C = float(np.max(q))
    L = len(q)

    B = build_derivative_matrix(h, n)
    if max_rows is not None and L > max_rows:
        order = np.argsort(q)[::-1]
        sel = order[:max_rows]
    else:
        sel = np.arange(L)

    A_rows = (B.T[sel] * ndx)
    b = A_rows.dot(h) - q[sel]

    m = len(sel)
    A_ub = np.empty((m, n + 1), dtype=float)
    A_ub[:, :n] = A_rows
    A_ub[:, n] = -1.0
    b_ub = b

    A_eq = np.ones((1, n + 1), dtype=float)
    A_eq[0, n] = 0.0
    b_eq = np.array([0.5 * n], dtype=float)

    c = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
    c[n] = 1.0

    lower = np.clip(h - delta, 0.0, 1.0)
    upper = np.clip(h + delta, 0.0, 1.0)
    bounds = [(float(lower[i]), float(upper[i])) for i in range(n)]
    bounds.append((0.0, float(C + 1.0)))

    sol = None
    for meth in ("highs", "highs-ds", None):
        try:
            if meth is None:
                sol = linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds)
            else:
                sol = linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq,
                              bounds=bounds, method=meth)
            if sol is not None and getattr(sol, "success", False):
                break
        except Exception:
            sol = None
            continue

    if sol is None or not getattr(sol, "success", False):
        return None, None

    h_new = np.asarray(sol.x[:n], dtype=float)
    h_new = adjust_in_box(h_new, lower, upper, n)
    if h_new is None:
        h_new = project_box_sum(h_new, n)
    h_new = snap_sum(h_new, n)
    c_new = compute_c5(h_new, n)
    return h_new, c_new


def slp_refine(h, n, deadline, rng=None, initial_delta=0.12,
               min_delta=1e-5, max_iters=5000, max_rows=None):
    if n <= 0:
        return h, compute_c5(h, 0)
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.RandomState(123456)

    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    C = compute_c5(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_C = C

    delta = float(initial_delta)
    stall = 0

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        if delta < min_delta:
            break

        res = slp_step(h, n, delta, max_rows=max_rows)
        if res is None or res[0] is None:
            delta *= 0.5
            continue

        h_try, C_try = res

        if C_try >= C - 1e-12:
            best_trial_h = h_try
            best_trial_C = C_try
            for fr in (0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625, 0.03125):
                if time.time() > deadline:
                    break
                h_f = h + fr * (h_try - h)
                h_f = project_box_sum(h_f, n)
                h_f = snap_sum(h_f, n)
                C_f = compute_c5(h_f, n)
                if C_f < best_trial_C:
                    best_trial_h = h_f
                    best_trial_C = C_f
                if C_f < C - 1e-12:
                    h_try = h_f
                    C_try = C_f
                    break
            else:
                if best_trial_C < C - 1e-12:
                    h_try = best_trial_h
                    C_try = best_trial_C

        if C_try < C - 1e-12:
            h = h_try
            C = C_try
            if C < best_C:
                best_C = C
                best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
            delta = max(min_delta, delta * 0.82)
        else:
            stall += 1
            delta *= 0.5
            if stall > 12 and best_C < np.inf:
                ns = max(0.004, min(0.02, delta * 0.25 + 0.002))
                h_p = best_h + ns * rng.randn(n)
                h = project_box_sum(h_p, n)
                h = snap_sum(h, n)
                C = compute_c5(h, n)
                delta = max(min_delta, min(0.06, ns * 2.0))
                stall = 0

    best_h = snap_sum(project_box_sum(best_h, n), n)
    best_C = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_C


def smooth_value(h, n, alpha):
    ndx = 2.0 / n
    q = np.asarray(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full"), dtype=float) * ndx
    m = float(q.max())
    sh = alpha * (q - m)
    e = np.exp(sh)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return m
    return m + float(np.log(s)) / alpha


def lbfgb_fun(x, n, alpha, lam):
    val = smooth_value(x, n, alpha)
    r = float(np.sum(x)) - 0.5 * n
    return val + lam * r * r


def lbfgb_jac(x, n, alpha, lam):
    ndx = 2.0 / n
    q = np.asarray(np.correlate(x, 1.0 - x, mode="full"), dtype=float) * ndx
    m = float(q.max())
    sh = alpha * (q - m)
    e = np.exp(sh)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros_like(q)
        w[int(np.argmax(q))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = e / s

    B = build_derivative_matrix(x, n)
    g = (B.T * ndx).dot(w)
    r = float(np.sum(x)) - 0.5 * n
    if lam != 0.0:
        g += (2.0 * lam * r) * np.ones_like(g)
    return g


def lbfgs_polish(h, n, deadline):
    if n <= 0:
        return h, compute_c5(h, 0)
    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    C = compute_c5(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_C = C
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n

    for alpha in (80.0, 400.0, 2000.0):
        for lam in (1.0, 100.0, 10000.0):
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            maxiter = 120
            try:
                res = minimize(
                    lbfgb_fun,
                    h,
                    method="L-BFGS-B",
                    jac=lbfgb_jac,
                    args=(n, alpha, lam),
                    bounds=bounds,
                    options={"maxiter": maxiter, "ftol": 1e-18, "gtol": 1e-10, "maxls": 20},
                )
            except Exception:
                continue

            h_new = project_box_sum(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float), n)
            h_new = snap_sum(h_new, n)
            c_new = compute_c5(h_new, n)
            if c_new < C - 1e-14:
                h = h_new
                C = c_new
            if c_new < best_C:
                best_C = c_new
                best_h = h_new.copy()

    best_h = snap_sum(project_box_sum(best_h, n), n)
    best_C = compute_c5(best_h, n)
    return best_h, best_C


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    eff = max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    deadline = t0 + eff
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).reshape(-1).copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        n = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n = 250
        parent = np.full(n, 0.5, dtype=float)
        parent += 0.05 * rng.randn(n)

    h0 = project_box_sum(parent, n)
    h0 = snap_sum(h0, n)
    c0 = compute_c5(h0, n)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_C = float(c0)
    best_n = int(n)

    def _update_if_better(hh, cc, nn):
        nonlocal best_h, best_C, best_n
        hh = np.asarray(hh, dtype=float)
        nn = int(nn)
        if hh.size != nn:
            return
        cc = float(compute_c5(hh, nn))
        if cc < best_C:
            best_h = hh.copy()
            best_C = cc
            best_n = nn

    # Main SLP refinement on the parent resolution.
    end1 = t0 + 0.34 * eff
    if time.time() < end1:
        h1, c1 = slp_refine(best_h, best_n, end1, rng=rng,
                            initial_delta=0.12, max_iters=8000)
        _update_if_better(h1, c1, best_n)

    # Binary basin search from the current fractional best.
    end2 = t0 + 0.50 * eff
    if time.time() < end2:
        if best_n % 2 == 0:
            bn = best_n
            bb = make_binary(best_h, bn)
        else:
            bn = best_n + 1
            bb = make_binary(resize_h(best_h, best_n, bn), bn)
        hb, cb = binary_local_search(bb, bn, end2, rng=rng)
        _update_if_better(hb, cb, bn)

        # SLP from the improved binary/fractional point.
        end3 = t0 + 0.64 * eff
        if time.time() < end3:
            h3, c3 = slp_refine(best_h, best_n, end3, rng=rng,
                                initial_delta=0.08, max_iters=8000)
            _update_if_better(h3, c3, best_n)

    # L-BFGS polish.
    end4 = t0 + 0.75 * eff
    if time.time() < end4:
        h4, c4 = lbfgs_polish(best_h, best_n, end4)
        _update_if_better(h4, c4, best_n)

    # Multi-resolution exploration.
    end5 = t0 + 0.91 * eff
    for nh in (300, 350, 200, 400):
        if time.time() > end5 - 20:
            break
        if nh != best_n and nh % 2 == 0:
            slice_end = min(end5, time.time() + 45.0)
            hh = resize_h(best_h, best_n, nh)

            # Quick discrete refinement in the new grid.
            if time.time() < slice_end - 10:
                hb = make_binary(hh, nh)
                hbl, cbl = binary_local_search(hb, nh, min(slice_end, time.time() + 15.0), rng=rng)
                _update_if_better(hbl, cbl, nh)

            # Fractional SLP in the new grid.
            if time.time() < slice_end:
                hh2 = resize_h(best_h, best_n, nh)
                hsl, csl = slp_refine(hh2, nh, slice_end, rng=rng,
                                      initial_delta=0.08, max_iters=2500,
                                      max_rows=700)
                _update_if_better(hsl, csl, nh)

    # Final continuous polish.
    if time.time() < deadline:
        h6, c6 = slp_refine(best_h, best_n, deadline, rng=rng,
                            initial_delta=0.03, max_iters=10000)
        _update_if_better(h6, c6, best_n)

    # Final quick discrete polish if feasible.
    if best_n % 2 == 0 and time.time() < deadline - 2.0:
        hb = make_binary(best_h, best_n)
        hbl, cbl = binary_local_search(hb, best_n, deadline, rng=rng)
        _update_if_better(hbl, cbl, best_n)

    best_h = snap_sum(project_box_sum(best_h, best_n), best_n)
    best_C = compute_c5(best_h, best_n)
    return best_h, float(best_C), int(best_n)
```
